# CTCAE v5.0 to v6.0 Mapping: Injury, poisoning and procedural complications

Verbatim extract of the sheet "CTCAE v5.0 to v6.0 Mapping" of the official CTCAE v6.0 workbook (https://dctd.cancer.gov/research/ctep-trials/trial-development/ctcae-v6.0.xlsx), rows whose "CTCAE v5.0 MedDRA 20.1 SOC" is "Injury, poisoning and procedural complications". Retrieved 2026-11-08. The official version prevails. Terms of use, source details and conversion notes: ../ctcae-v6-vs-v5-changes.md.

Rows: 447; CTCAE v5.0 terms: 79.

### Ankle fracture

CTCAE v5.0 MedDRA 20.1 LLT Code: 10002544

- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Definition | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** A finding of damage to the ankle joint characterized by a break in the continuity of the ankle bone. Symptoms include marked discomfort, swelling and difficulty moving the affected leg and foot.
  - **Change Type:** Deletion
  - **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 LLT Code:** 10017076 | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 SOC:** Injury, poisoning and procedural complications | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 Term:** Fracture
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Definition | **CTCAE v6.0 Description:** A state in which the continuity of the bone is broken.
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Other Specify:** N/A
- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 1 | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** Mild; non-operative intervention indicated
  - **Change Type:** Deletion
  - **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 LLT Code:** 10017076 | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 SOC:** Injury, poisoning and procedural complications | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 Term:** Fracture
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 1 | **CTCAE v6.0 Description:** Asymptomatic; clinical or diagnostic observations only; intervention not indicated
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Other Specify:** N/A
- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 2 | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** Limiting instrumental ADL; outpatient operative intervention indicated
  - **Change Type:** Deletion
  - **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 LLT Code:** 10017076 | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 SOC:** Injury, poisoning and procedural complications | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 Term:** Fracture
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 2 | **CTCAE v6.0 Description:** Symptomatic but non-displaced; immobilization indicated
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Other Specify:** N/A
- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 3 | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** Limiting self care ADL; elective operative intervention indicated requiring hospitalization
  - **Change Type:** Deletion
  - **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 LLT Code:** 10017076 | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 SOC:** Injury, poisoning and procedural complications | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 Term:** Fracture
  - **CTCAE v6.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Grade 3 | **CTCAE v6.0 Description:** Severe symptoms; displaced or open wound with bone exposure; operative intervention indicated; limiting self-care ADL or severe impact on age-appropriate normal daily activity (pediatric)

### Burn

CTCAE v5.0 MedDRA 20.1 LLT Code: 10006634

- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Definition | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** A finding of impaired integrity to the anatomic site of an adverse thermal reaction. Burns can be caused by exposure to chemicals, direct heat, electricity, flames and radiation. The extent of damage depends on the length and intensity of exposure and time until provision of treatment.

### Radiation recall reaction (dermatologic)

CTCAE v5.0 MedDRA 20.1 LLT Code: 10037767

- **CTCAE v5.0 Grade/Definition/Navigational Note:** Definition | **CTCAE v5.0 Description:** A finding of acute skin inflammatory reaction caused by drugs, especially chemotherapeutic agents, for weeks or months following radiotherapy. The inflammatory reaction is confined to the previously irradiated skin and the symptoms disappear after the removal of the pharmaceutical agent.
  - **Change Type:** No change
  - **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 LLT Code:** 10037767 | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 SOC:** Injury, poisoning and procedural complications | **CTCAE v6.0 MedDRA 28.0 Term:** Radiation recall reaction (dermatologic)

The inflammatory reaction is confined to the previously irradiated skin and the symptoms disappear after the removal of the pharmaceutical agent.
